I don’t feel out of control like that.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating, even if it's not a huge amount, do you ever feel like you can't stop eating or control what or how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think so. I mean, I can stop if I need to. It’s more just… I eat without thinking much, sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset, ashamed, or distressed about your eating habits or the times when you eat in a way that doesn’t feel normal to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it bothers me. It’s not like I’m ashamed exactly, but my eating’s been… off for months. Some days it’s just toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing there buying sweets I don’t even really want. It feels wrong, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or do anything else to try to prevent gaining weight after eating those sweets or on days when you feel like you've eaten more than you intended?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t make myself sick or anything like that. I don’t really do anything after.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating those sweets or on days when your eating feels off, do you feel like you can't control yourself or that it's happening automatically, even if you could stop if you really wanted to?\n\nPatient: I guess… it can feel a bit automatic, yeah. Not like I’m completely out of control, but my eating’s been all turned around for months. Some days I barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really want.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating those sweets or eating more than you plan when you’re feeling stressed, sad, or upset?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, I guess. I don’t always know if it’s because I’m upset… it’s just been all turned around for months. Some days it’s only toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat sweets or find yourself eating in a way that feels out of control in private because you're embarrassed about it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe a little. I don’t really like anyone seeing it. It’s just that my eating’s been all over the place for months—some days barely toast and coffee, and other days I’m standing there buying sweets I don’t even want.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Some days I barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really want.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating those sweets or eating more than you plan when you’re feeling stressed, sad, or upset?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, I guess. I don’t always know if it’s because I’m upset… it’s just been all turned around for months. Some days it’s only toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat sweets or find yourself eating in a way that feels out of control in private because you're embarrassed about it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe a little. I don’t really like anyone seeing it. It’s just that my eating’s been all over the place for months—some days barely toast and coffee, and other days I’m standing there buying sweets I don’t even want.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating much more rapidly than usual during those times when your eating feels off?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure about rapidly. It’s more that my eating’s been all turned around for months… some days I’m barely having toast and coffee, and then other days I’m just there buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat sweets or eat in that off way when you're alone, or do you feel okay doing it in front of others?\n\nPatient: Mostly alone, I think.
